rotations and contractions at all earlier levels.

A broader finite-depth obstruction uses only a few seed features. Consider
ANY nonnegative seed word p of capacity A_0=1+sum p_i with an actual
projection core, initial norm R>0, endpoint w, and
k_0=<x_0,w>/R^2>0. Suppose one of its samples has g_i=gamma*w and
y_i=delta*w with gamma,delta>0. Use the same unlifted isometric copying
operation with balanced bridges r_j=theta lambda^j A_0, allowing every
transverse map at every join. At the first join the GD identity gives

    sum p_i g_right,i + g_right,terminal = -r_0 w.

The nonnegative weights sum to A_0. Their weighted average of the signed
initial/gradient correlations is therefore -r_0 k_0/A_0=-theta k_0.
Embedding this whole first join always into the left child preserves
those correlations. Separately, embedding the displayed endpoint-facing
sample always into the right child preserves g_i=gamma*w and
y_i=delta*w relative to the current endpoint. At a later top join (15),
with the factor gamma retained, requires every right correlation to be
at least -gamma/r_j. Averaging them forces

    r_j theta k_0 <= gamma.                             (32)

Because r_j grows as lambda^j and k_0>0, this inequality eventually fails.
Thus this larger class of FIXED seeds with positive initial/endpoint
pairing and one such residual cannot be iterated indefinitely solely by
choosing transverse correlations. The statement uses projection values;
the independent value-rigidity argument in section 5 covers arbitrary
value selection for the specific orthogonal seeds of the main claim.
It does not cover seeds without the displayed positive pairing or
endpoint-facing sample, depth-dependent changes of seed data, or lifts.

For a general feasible join, the signed boundary pairings transport as

    <g_left,W>=alpha B_C(g_L,w_R),
    <y_left,W>=alpha B_C(y_L,w_R),
    <g_right,W>=alpha^2<g_R,w_R>,
    <y_right,W>=alpha^2<y_R,w_R>,
    <x_parent,0,g_left>=<x_L,0,g_L>,
    <x_parent,0,y_left>=<x_L,0,y_L>,
    <x_parent,0,g_right>=alpha B_C(x_L,0,g_R),
    <x_parent,0,y_right>=alpha B_C(x_L,0,y_R).            (31)

The cross entries of the full retained Gram are (9), with factor alpha
when exactly one vector comes from the right child. Internal right
entries have factor alpha^2. Equations (9)-(16),(31) give an exact full
coupling rule at any growing horizon WHEN feasible. They assert no
iteration theorem without proving feasibility at every step. Here the
concrete obstruction (3) decides it: arbitrary transverse freedom alone
does not make this seed family iterable.

## 7. Discovery ledger, calibration, and evidence preservation

The three recorded optimization evaluations all used t=1/100 and the
same explicit seed (5). The smaller calibration was h[2] of length 15,
whose legality is proved in section 3. Both h[4] cases used the SAME
actual h[3] children and the SAME h[4] word.

1. Length 15, C fixed to zero: numerical normalized residual objective
   8.7710378984e-11, maximum original Q about 8.33e-17.
2. Length 63, C fixed to zero: objective 0.4164822178621603;
   Q_(32,25) approximately 0.01256893369169749.
3. Length 63, full unrestricted 8 by 8 C: objective
   0.41648221754700987; largest dual weights are the duplicate original
   rows (36,30),(37,30). The contraction block is strictly PSD in this
   numerical minimax-residual solution, with smallest eigenvalue about
   0.597687. It is NOT a feasible interpolant: the positive residuals
   were neither discarded nor repaired.

The common exact predicted normalized row residual is lambda T(xi)-1,
approximately 0.416482217535. Discovery used 3 of 8 allowed optimization
evaluations and 18.623366069994517 measured build-plus-solve seconds of
60, including imports, model construction, and solver compilation. There
were 2 distinct seed/parent cases out of the allowed 4. Each attempt was
logged BEFORE evaluation and updated with actual cost afterward.

All 4,225 rows of the 63-step ordered-pair/optimum/diagonal matrix were
kept, along with full contraction PSD. The positive row scales alter only
the residual objective, not the feasibility set. Raw child coordinates,
all words, orthonormal bases, all original Q values, the entire C, all Q
dual entries, the PSD dual, the scalar dual, and complete contraction
eigenvalues are saved. No eigenvalue was clipped, no PSD repair applied,
and no nullspace deleted. Source and raw-file SHA256 hashes are recorded
in the public ledger. The executable source is
workers/max/research/max_general_transverse_discovery.py; raw files are
workers/max/research/transverse_raw/attempt_01 through attempt_03 (.npz
and .json). The ledger is research/max_general_transverse_discovery_budget.json.
The separate tiny exact audit max_transverse_exact_audit.py passed 19
checks in Q(sqrt(2)), including the seed formulas, both separator signs,
the factorization (29), and the two polynomial identities used in the
legality proof. It makes no optimization or verification call. A read-only
inspection of the saved arrays gave the identical invariant Q_(36,30)
in both length-63 runs and the two-row residual approximately
0.00256492296668. These are diagnostic consistency checks, not acceptance
by the proof verifier.

The derivations in sections 1-6 use no numerical output as a premise.
No installation, verifier call, additional solver replay, external
publication, or subagent was used. No allowance renews. This document is
an unverified proof candidate, and the project's original target remains
unresolved.

## 8. Scope test: a changed actual seed delays this particular separator

This is a separate construction on the SAME three-step word (5), not a
replacement of the fixed children in the preceding discovery comparisons.
It sharpens the scope of the result; it is NOT a legal 63-step repair.
There was no optimization evaluation on this changed seed.

Keep the exact a,b of (5), and set

    rho=599/600, Klen=a+rho, z=b-1, m=a+b,
    eta=z/Klen, Rnew^2=m^2+Klen*z, A=1+2a+b.

Take orthogonal U,V with ||U||^2=1 and ||V||^2=eta, and define

    (x_0,g_0)=(mU+Klen V,U),
    (x_1,g_1)=(bU+Klen V,U),
    (x_2,g_2)=(Klen V,V), (x_3,g_3)=(rho V,rho V).      (33)

The residuals are y_0=(m-1)U+Klen V, y_1=zU+Klen V,
y_2=(Klen-1)V, y_3=0. The three GD updates have steps a,b,a.
Here Klen>1 and 0<rho<1. At samples 0 and 1 the normals against V are
-a and 0, and the normals against 0 are -(m-1),-z. Normals against rho V
are no larger than those against V because all the V/residual pairings
are nonnegative. At sample 2 the normals against U and 0 equal
-(Klen-1)eta, and the normal against rho V equals
-(1-rho)(Klen-1)eta. At sample 3 all residuals vanish. Normals against
each sample's own gradient vanish. This checks every distinct vertex of
conv(0,U,V,rho V). Section 2 supplies the actual common function, values,
and TRUE distance Rnew, since x_0=rho V+(a+b)U+aV is a nonnegative
combination of the actual gradients.

This seed still has positive defect, with

    kappa_new=A^2 rho^2 eta/Rnew^2 >1+1/2000.            (34)

It also has an explicit legal first transverse join and an optimal signed
initial/gradient minimum at that join. Take r_0=theta A and use transverse
bases U on the left and
(Klen sqrt(eta)/Rnew, -m/Rnew) on the right in the orthonormal coordinates
(U,V/sqrt(eta)). The latter vector is perpendicular to x_0/Rnew and has
norm one. Let the scalar cross map be c in [0,1]. The scaled physical
cross matrix on the pair (U,V) is exactly

    r_0 rho eta/Rnew^2 * [[Klen*c, -m*c],[-m,-z]].       (35)

This follows directly from (9) with w=rho V and
alpha=r_0 rho sqrt(eta)/Rnew. It is realized by (10), with scalar
S_C=sqrt(1-c^2), so no PSD correction is implicit.

All cross gradient/residual inner products in BOTH directions are
nonpositive. To check the only possibly positive terms, remove the
positive prefactor in (35). Pair a right U with the three nonzero left
residuals; the results are

    Klen[(m-1)c-m], Klen[zc-m], -m(Klen-1),

which are nonpositive because c<=1 and m-z=a+1>0. Pair a left U with
the three nonzero right residuals; the results are

    -Klen*c, -Klen*(a+1)*c, -m*c*(Klen-1),

which are nonpositive because c>=0. Every remaining pair uses the V row
or column of (35), whose entries are nonpositive, with nonnegative
residual coefficients; rho V contributes an additional positive factor.
The fourth residuals are zero. Subtracting the nonnegative energies in
(13) checks every cross Q. Thus this is an actual legal seven-step join
for EVERY c in [0,1], with true distance Rnew and ratio kappa_new^2.

The retained signed correlations of its right U,V gradients are

    c_U=r_0 rho*m*z*(c-1)/Rnew^4,
    c_V=-r_0 rho*eta*(m^2 c+Klen*z)/Rnew^4.

Choose

    c_opt=(a+1)Klen/[m(m+Klen)] in (0,1).               (36)

Then c_U=c_V=-r_0 k_new/(m+Klen), where k_new=rho*z/Rnew^2.
The right rho V gradient has correlation rho*c_V and is less negative;
the left gradients have positive initial correlations. Equation
m*g_right,U+Klen*g_right,V=-r_0 w proves that their weighted average
has this value for ANY transverse coupling, so (36) maximizes the
minimum signed initial/gradient correlation over every allowed coupling
at the first join. It is an exact argument, not a numerical optimizer.

The last queried gradient in (33) is w/rho, rather than w. Thus the
endpoint-facing row threshold (30) becomes c_R,j>=-1/(rho*r). The
positive weighted combination inherited from these two first-join
gradients would, at the fourth balanced bridge r_3=theta lambda^3 A,
have normalized residual Charge_new-1, where

    Charge_new=r_3 rho*r_0 k_new/(m+Klen)<1-1/4000.     (37)

Consequently that inherited two-row certificate is strictly NEGATIVE
for this changed actual seed; it cannot exclude the 63-step parent.
Neither existence of legal intervening 15-,31-step general-coupling
children nor feasibility of its 63-step parent is asserted here.

For a mechanically checkable sign audit of (34),(37), their exact
expressions in Q(sqrt(2)) are

    kappa_new-1 =
      [-19019047380837966731+13474265781546157318*s]
          /40842989603870102500,

    1-Charge_new =
      [341554841617983701-241357907739927883*s]
          /603687956995287500.

For the first margin, write the displayed positive integers in its
numerator and denominator as P1,Q1,D1, so the numerator is -P1+Q1*s.
Then

    2(2000 Q1)^2-(2000 P1+D1)^2
       =2441206548123238434623351342332719331750000>0.

Both compared quantities are positive, so this proves (34). For the
second margin write its numerator as P2-Q2*s and its denominator as D2.
The quantity 4000 P2-D2 is positive, and

    (4000 P2-D2)^2-2(4000 Q2)^2
       =789713290761361657120990345679724250000>0.

This proves (37). The seven exact sign/formula checks are recorded in
max_transverse_changed_seed_scope_audit.json. This is trivial scalar
arithmetic, not a parameter sweep, conic evaluation, or verification call.

Finally, this example does NOT evade (32): it still has k_new>0 and the
endpoint-facing constants gamma=1/rho and delta=(Klen-1)/rho>0. Fixed
copies therefore fail at some later depth under arbitrary transverse
coupling. The example demonstrates a real scope distinction between the
specific 63-step separator and the broader eventual obstruction, and
identifies why changing only this scalar terminal ratio is insufficient
for an all-depth construction.

# Robust nonstationary positive-value relay with exponential growth
Status: complete explicit construction, UNVERIFIED; no new verifier allocation.
This extends the periodic signed-history example by allowing independently varying modulation at every stage and a strictly growing gradient scale. All proof ingredients are included here.

## 1. Schedule class and value
Fix
a=9/10, T=23/10, kappa=1/10, chi=6/5, q_g=901/900.
Choose any m>=1 and ANY numbers v_i in[1,chi],0<=i<=m, with v_0=1.
Define alpha_i=q_g^i v_i and
b_i=T-1+(T/a)alpha_(i+1)/alpha_i,0<=i<m.
The supplied schedule is h=(1,b_0,...,1,b_(m-1)), of length2m.
Every b_i belongs to[3,5]; these are variable checkpoints, not a constant schedule.

Then one explicitly defined nonseparable convex1-smooth function in R^(m+1) gives
W_(2m)(h)>=(85/121) q_g^(2m) v_m^2
          >=(85/121)(901/900)^(2m).                     (1)
Its exact closest-minimizer normalization is used. Meanwhile the entire quadratic-class objective is at most
max{1/(2 e m),(4/5)^(2m)} ->0.                           (2)
Thus the nonlinear error grows exponentially on a robust nonstationary class with freely chosen v_i.

Equivalently, this is an explicit condition on a supplied checkpoint schedule: the multiplicative partial products
v_i=product_(j=0)^(i-1) [(9/(23 q_g))(b_j-13/10)]
must all lie in[1,6/5]. The empty product v_0=1 is fixed. This condition is sufficient, not asserted for arbitrary schedules.

## 2. One global function and exact residual identity
Let zeta_0=e_0 and
zeta_(i+1)=-a zeta_i+sqrt(1-a^2)e_(i+1).
Their Gram matrix is real PSD with
<zeta_i,zeta_j>=(-a)^|i-j|.
Set omega_i=alpha_i zeta_i and
J(z)=max{0,max_i(<omega_i,z>-kappa alpha_i^2)}, f=env_1 J.
This is one finite nonnegative convex max-affine function, so its envelope is convex, differentiable and1-smooth, with minimum0. The direct proximal optimality condition proves that if plane i is active at x-omega_i then
grad f(x)=omega_i, f(x)=<omega_i,x>-(1/2+kappa)alpha_i^2.
Subgradient monotonicity gives the1-Lipschitz gradient estimate, ensuring full global legality, not only local interpolation.

Start at X_0=T alpha_0 zeta_0=T zeta_0, and set the intended block starts
X_(i+1)=X_i-(1+b_i)omega_i.
Let Z_i=X_i-T omega_i. Define
B_i=(T/a)alpha_(i+1), D_i=B_i(1-a^2).
Then
Z_0=0, Z_(i+1)=Z_i-B_i(zeta_i+a zeta_(i+1)).
Consequently
<Z_i,zeta_j>=0 for j>=i,
<Z_i,zeta_j>=-S_(j,i) for j<i,
S_(j,i)=sum_(l=j)^(i-1)D_l(-a)^(l-j).                   (3)
This follows by taking inner products of the update: zeta_l+a zeta_(l+1) is orthogonal to every later zeta_j, and its earlier inner products are(1-a^2)(-a)^(l-j).

At either real query x=X_i-u omega_i,u=0,1, the proposed proximal point is Z_i+d omega_i with d=T-u-1. The terminal query has d=T-1. Put delta=T-2=3/10, so all these d are>=delta.
The current plane has value(d-kappa)alpha_i^2>=alpha_i^2/5>0.
For an old index j its deficit relative to the current plane is
d alpha_i(alpha_i-C_ij alpha_j)
 +alpha_j S_(j,i)-kappa alpha_i^2+kappa alpha_j^2,        (4)
where C_ij=(-a)^(i-j). Future indices have the same expression with S=0.

## 3. Uniform estimates for the signed history
Put theta=a q_g=901/1000 and K=(T/a)(1-a^2)=437/900.
For L=i-j>0,
S_(j,i)/q_g^j=K q_g sum_(r=0)^(L-1)(-theta)^r v_(j+r+1).
Define the negative constant
S_floor=K q_g(1-theta chi)/(1-theta^2).
Using v in[1,chi] on positive and negative terms gives:
if L is even,
S_(j,i)/q_g^j >= S_floor(1-theta^L);                    (5)
if L is odd,
S_(j,i)/q_g^j >= S_floor
 +K q_g theta^(L-1)(theta chi-theta^2)/(1-theta^2)
 >=S_floor.                                           (6)
Thus old residuals may be positive; only their exact quantitative cost is bounded.

The following rational bounds will suffice:
theta^2<13/16, theta^2>4/5,
theta chi-1<13/160,
K q_g<39/80,
|S_floor|<169/800.                                     (7)
For the last inequality divide the product of the preceding bounds by
1-theta^2>3/16:
(39/80)(13/160)/(3/16)=169/800.
All numbers here are exact finite rational inequalities.

Every coefficient of d in(4), including future comparisons, is positive.
For even separation in a past comparison,
C_ij alpha_j/alpha_i=(a/q_g)^L v_j/v_i<=a^2 chi<1.
For a future even separation the ratio is
theta^L v_j/v_i<=theta^2 chi<39/40<1.
Odd correlations are negative. Therefore checking d=delta suffices.

Future planes pass for arbitrary amplitude ratios. At even separation their deficit at d=delta is
(delta-kappa)alpha_i^2+kappa alpha_j^2-delta C_ij alpha_i alpha_j.
This quadratic form is positive definite because
delta^2 C_ij^2<=delta^2 a^4=59049/1000000
 <4kappa(delta-kappa)=80000/1000000.
At odd separation every term has a nonnegative sign.

For past comparisons normalize(4) by q_g^(2j). Set
z=q_g^L v_i>=v_i>=1, y=v_j in[1,chi].
If L is odd, (6) and the positive cross term give the lower bound
(delta-kappa)z^2+kappa y^2+S_floor y
 >=3/10-(169/800)(6/5)=93/2000>0.                       (8)

If L is even, the part
(delta-kappa)z^2-delta a^L z y
increases with z>=1, since its derivative is at least
2/5-(3/10)a^2 chi>0.
Replace z by v_i; then replace a^L by the larger theta^L to lower the expression further. Using(5), the result is
F(v_i,y,w)=(delta-kappa)v_i^2+kappa y^2
 -delta w v_i y+S_floor(1-w)y,
where w=theta^L lies in[0,theta^2].
This is affine in w, so only endpoints need checking.
At w=0, the estimate in(8) applies.
At w=theta^2, F increases with v_i on[1,chi]:
2(delta-kappa)v_i-delta theta^2 y
 >=2/5-(3/10)(13/16)(6/5)>0.
It decreases with y on that interval, since
2kappa y-delta theta^2 v_i+S_floor(1-theta^2)
 <6/25-(3/10)(4/5)=0.
Its minimum is therefore at v_i=1,y=chi. By(7),
F(1,chi,theta^2)
>43/125-(3/10)(13/16)(6/5)-(39/80)(13/160)(6/5)
=127/32000>0.                                          (9)
This proves every old-plane comparison at every lag, including those with S<0.

The zero, current, past and future checks establish all prescribed gradients. In particular at each checkpoint
J(X_i-2omega_i)=alpha_i^2/5>0,
so the new mechanism carries a positive proximal value rather than resetting to the minimum.

## 4. Exact minimizer distance and objective
The minimizer set equals
P_min={z:<omega_i,z><=kappa alpha_i^2 for all i}.
Because alpha_0=1 and alpha_i>=1, y_proj=kappa zeta_0 belongs to P_min: odd correlations are negative; even ones are at most1. Constraint0 is active and
X_0-y_proj=(T-kappa)omega_0.
This is a nonnegative active-normal certificate, so y_proj is the exact closest minimizer. The squared initial distance is
(T-kappa)^2=(11/5)^2=121/25.
The exact terminal value is
2f(X_m)=2(T-kappa-1/2)alpha_m^2=(17/5)alpha_m^2.
Dividing gives(1).

The gradients omega_0,...,omega_m are independent with adjacent nonzero correlations, and their squared norms alpha_i^2 vary. Independence allows each zero/plane hinge to be isolated; its envelope Hessian is the rank-one projector onto omega_i. Adjacent projectors do not commute, proving nonseparability in every fixed orthonormal basis.

## 5. Actual negative history and the quadratic comparison
This family strictly escapes the old sign restriction. Choose any eligible consecutive modulation pattern
v_j=chi,v_(j+1)=1,v_(j+2)=chi.
Then
S_(j,j+2)=K q_g^(j+1)(1-theta chi)<0.
Nevertheless(8)--(9) certify the full old plane. The construction therefore does not merely hide a nonpositive-residual assumption behind new notation.

The checkpoints obey
13/10+(23/9)q_g(5/6)<=b_i<=13/10+(23/9)q_g(6/5),
an interval contained in[3,5]. For the quadratic multiplier
(1-lambda)(1-b_i lambda),
on0<=lambda<=1/5 it is nonnegative and<=exp(-lambda); on1/5<=lambda<=1 its absolute value is<=4/5 by the endpoints and its single quadratic vertex. Thus spectral diagonalization gives(2) for every finite-dimensional convex quadratic.

This is a rigorous candidate lower bound for the displayed nonstationary class, not the exact target theorem. The bounded-modulation/partial-product condition is explicitly retained; no claim says arbitrary schedules satisfy it or can be partitioned into such blocks without losing the target constant.

# P5 extension: exact history-aware plateau actions
Status: new complete construction and quantitative tests; unverified. No new verifier allocation requested. The uniform exact-profile comparison is still BLOCKED.

## 1. History and orientation
Work at L=1, minimizer0, initial point1. A finite history H contains(0,0) and gradient knots(y,v) satisfying 0<=v-w<=y-z whenever y>=z. Define
ell_H(r)=max_(y,v in H)[v-(y-r)_+],
u_H(r)=min_(y,v in H)[v+(r-y)_+].
The extrema of nondecreasing1-Lipschitz functions are again nondecreasing1-Lipschitz. The history inequalities show that both envelopes interpolate every knot. Hence a new gradient at r is legal exactly in[ell_H(r),u_H(r)]. Integrating either envelope gives a global convex1-smooth function with minimizer0. For a completed path ending at x, its largest possible normalized value is
Phi(H,x)=2 integral_0^x u_H(r)dr for x>=0,
Phi(H,x)=-2 integral_x^0 ell_H(r)dr for x<=0.
This follows by the pointwise envelope inequalities and is attained by integrating the indicated entire envelope. Every previous queried gradient is preserved.

For a nonzero current point, reflect if necessary so that it becomes a positive radius x: replace every knot(y,v) by(sigma y,sigma v), where sigma is the sign of the current point. All formulas below concern this reflected positive coordinate. Multiply selected magnitudes by sigma to obtain the original gradient. At x=0 choose gradient0.

## 2. Exact feasibility of a constant-gradient future block
Fix a nonnegative proposed block mass S. A magnitude q>=0 can be imposed on the entire interval[x-Sq,x] consistently with the old history exactly when
q belongs to I(H,x,S):=[ell_H(x), C(H,x,S)],
where
C(H,x,S)=min_(y,v in H) max{v,(x-y+v)/(1+S)}.                 (1)
The interval may be empty.

To prove this, add endpoint knots(x,q) and(z,q), where z=x-Sq. Monotonicity of the envelopes makes their joint feasibility equivalent to
q>=ell_H(x), q<=u_H(z).
The origin knot implies that the latter inequality with q>0 forces z>=q>0. For q=0, z=x>0. Thus the imposed plateau stays on the selected ray. For one old knot the upper inequality is
q<=v+(x-y-Sq)_+.
The continuous function on the right minus q is strictly decreasing, with unique zero
max{v,(x-y+v)/(1+S)}.
Indeed if x-y<=Sv the zero is v; otherwise it is(x-y+v)/(1+S). Intersecting these inequalities proves(1). The origin knot also gives ell_H(x)>=0 and C(H,x,S)<=x/(1+S).

Once both endpoint knots are feasible, the derivative has to be constant q between them; adding any intermediate knot with value q preserves legality. Hence every nonnegative step block of total mass S follows this plateau, in its actual chronological order. No individual stepsize bound is needed.

## 3. Exact optimal completion by a constant-gradient tail
For a tail of total mass S, any q in I(H,x,S) has exact maximal normalized terminal value
A(H,x,S;q)=2 integral_0^(x-Sq) min{u_H(r),q}dr.             (2)
Adding the two endpoint knots makes the new upper envelope on[0,x-Sq] exactly min{u_H(r),q}. Integrate that global new envelope. The resulting function attains(2), realizes the entire constant-gradient tail and every old gradient, and has initial squared distance1.

This optimum is a concrete finite calculation from the old knots. The function u_H is continuous piecewise affine with slopes0 or1 and finitely many pieces. On a rising piece write u_H(r)=r-d. For q whose level lies in that piece, put
E=integral_0^(q+d)u_H(r)dr-q^2/2;
E is constant as q varies through that rising piece. Formula(2) becomes
A=2E+2q(x-d)-(1+2S)q^2.                                 (3)
Its only interior stationary point is
q=(x-d)/(1+2S).                                          (4)
An optimizing q can therefore be selected by checking:
- the endpoints of the nonempty interval I(H,x,S);
- the finitely many plateau heights of u_H that belong to I;
- the values(4) whose level lies in the associated rising piece and in I.
The formula also covers zero levels by including the interval endpoints. Comparing these finitely many exact values selects q_stop(H,x,S). No numerical enumeration of trajectories or optimization over all functions is being assumed.

The offsets d and retained areas E in(3) are compulsory history data. The former proposal x/(1+2S) silently replaced d by0 and need not be optimal even when it is feasible.

## 4. A specified finite enlarged action tree
At time t, with current point x_t and full gradient history H_t, retain the old P5 actions:
(a) the signed steep envelope;
(b) the projection of x_t/(1+2S_t) onto the complete feasible interval, where S_t=sum_(r=t)^(n-1)h_r.

Add the following actions:
(c) the signed value q_stop for S=S_t, whenever I is nonempty;
(d) for each future checkpoint j in{t,...,n-1}, the signed value C(H,|x_t|,S_(t,j)), whenever I is nonempty, where
S_(t,j)=sum_(r=t)^(j-1)h_r.
History is reflected as in Section1 for(c),(d). Choose any listed action, add its knot, and apply the given h_t. At the end use Phi.

This is an explicit finite tree, with at most n+3 listed actions at each time, at most(n+3)^n leaves, and no free continuum of gradient choices. It starts from H_0={(0,0)}, x_0=1, rather than requiring a preselected seed. Every action is pathwise legal by the formulas above. Thus every completed leaf is a single globally defined legal one-dimensional function, and its displayed payoff is at most W_n(h).

When action(c) chooses a constant-tail magnitude q, the rest of that tail is present in the tree: choose the signed steep action thereafter. All its future radii lie in[z,x], where z=x-S_t q. The old upper envelope is at least q throughout this interval by feasibility at z, while the newly added knot(x,q) bounds the new upper envelope by q at all smaller radii. Hence every following signed steep choice is exactly q and realizes the completion in(2).

Let M_new(h) be the maximum leaf payoff. A genuine probability law whose expectation loses a uniformly vanishing factor is obtained by giving a leaf of value Z>=0 mass proportional to Z^(n^2), for n>=1. If all values vanish, use any law. With N leaves and p=n^2, Jensen's inequality for a->a^(1+1/p) gives
[sum Z^(p+1)]/[sum Z^p] >= [N^(-1)sum Z^p]^(1/p)
 >= N^(-1/p) max Z.
Consequently the expected normalized payoff T_new satisfies
(n+3)^(-1/n) M_new(h) <= T_new(h) <= M_new(h) <= W_n(h).   (5)
The factor tends to1 independently of h. This distribution can be sampled as conditional branch probabilities using sums of descendant pth powers; their products telescope to the leaf law. This is a symbolic specification, not a computation performed here. The schedule is fixed before the law or any function is chosen.

For n=0 take f(x)=x^2/2, with normalized error1.

## 5. Inclusion of the old policy and the accepted ray construction
The tree contains all original forced-prefix P5 leaves. Indeed choosing checkpoint j initially gives C=1/(1+P_j), where P_j=sum_(r<j)h_r. If q=C was chosen for a block of mass S and a first step of mass a<=S has been performed, q is still feasible for the residual block from x-aq. The new knot(x,q) gives the residual upper bound C_new<=q, while feasibility gives C_new>=q. Thus C_new=q. Repeating the same lookahead checkpoint keeps exactly the old constant-gradient prefix. At the checkpoint itself S=0, so C=u_H at the query, again equal to q. Thereafter the two original actions are retained.

It also contains the exact alternating-ray leaves in accepted fact1fca3e5dd555cb4a. At a new outward stage let(A_old,w_old) be the previous record on that ray, d=A_old-w_old, and let the current radius satisfy x=d+(1+S)m with m>=w_old. Every earlier same-ray knot(y,v) satisfies y-v<=d and v<=w_old. The opposite-ray knots satisfy y-v<=0<=d. Formula(1) therefore gives C>=m, while the record knot gives C<=max(w_old,(x-d)/(1+S))=m. Thus the future-checkpoint action is exactly m. The residual-block argument then follows every plateau in that fact. At the terminal stage the stored knots force the same old upper derivative envelope as its slope1 ramps and flat segments. Formula(3) has its exact retained area e_term and offset d; q_stop is precisely its q_opt. Therefore
M_new(h)>=F_ray_n(h)>=K_n(h)
using the accepted fact, without discarding any ray feasibility condition.

This inclusion is a comparison of constructions, not a second independent claim that the ray family solves the target. The added actions are also available at non-record states, and the old P5 inner-return branches are preserved.

## 6. Exact strict improvement over the best old P5 leaf
Take h=(1,5,3,1). In the old P5 tree, seed j=0 reaches0 at its first step; seed j=3 also reaches0 at the last step. Seed j=2 has c=1/7 and its best last-step leaf gives4/147. For seed j=1, c=1/2 and the points through its forced prefix are1,1/2,-2.

At -2 the old interior choice is -2/9, because the remaining mass is4. It stays on this plateau and yields4/9. The old steep choice is -2 and sends the point to4. At4, the old positive upper envelope is
u(r)=r on[0,1/2], 1/2 on[1/2,1], r-1/2 forr>=1.
The last old steep action takes gradient7/2 and ends at1/2, giving1/4. The last old interior action takes gradient4/3 and ends at8/3. Its maximal normalized value is
1/2+2(4/3)(7/2)-3(4/3)^2=9/2.
These cases exhaust the old tree, so its maximum leaf payoff is exactly9/2; its K_4 fallback is at most4/9.

At the same history, the new optimal tail uses d=1/2, E=1/4, S=1. Formula(4) gives q=7/6, which lies in I=[1/2,7/4]. The new terminal value is
1/2+(7/2)^2/3=55/12 >9/2.
A complete global realizing derivative is
q_global(x)=x for x<=1/2;
1/2 for1/2<=x<=1;
x-1/2 for1<=x<=5/3;
7/6 forx>=5/3.
It is continuous, nondecreasing and1-Lipschitz. Its trajectory is
1,1/2,-2,4,17/6,
and direct integration gives normalized error55/12. The terminal gain over9/2 is exactly1/12. This verifies a real geometric improvement, not a change in averaging weights.

## 7. Checkpoint lookahead test
For h=(1,5,1,9,1), choose the checkpoint5 from the initial state, giving magnitude1/2 through its prefix. At the resulting point-2 choose future checkpoint9; the ordinary intervening mass is1, so formula(1) gives magnitude1. The next two points are-1 and8. The exact tail optimizer at8 has d=1/2,E=1/4,S=1 and q=5/2, giving
2E+(8-d)^2/3=77/4.
This reproduces the accepted fact1fca3e5dd555cb4a example. The old two-action rule at-2 offered only magnitudes2 and2/23; neither was the needed1. The new action is derived from complete-history feasibility, not inserted to fit this example.

## Target status
No uniform comparison of M_new(h) with U_n^ZJ has been proved. Equation(5) removes probability-weight loss; the new formulas repair two concrete omissions of the geometry. A theorem that M_new(h)>=(1-o(1))U_n^ZJ uniformly for every finite nonnegative h would still be a target-strength theorem and remains BLOCKED. The finite examples above do not establish such a statement or an asymptotic negative answer.

# Correlated Moreau relay surviving arbitrary suffixes of linear length
Status: complete new constructive candidate, UNVERIFIED. No additional verifier call.
This responds to the fixed-witness two-step failure50b0bb4cd67cac9f and the quantifier reminder d7b99ad37dff6ae5. The adversary is replaced by a different correlated function on the SAME periodic prefix. The stronger uniform suffix property is proved below, not imposed as a requirement on all adversaries.

## 1. Precise statement
Fix rational constants
a_0=9/10, q=10001/10000, a=a_0 q,
theta=a q=a_0 q^2,
chi=6/5, T=23/10, kappa=1/10.
Define the integer
L_prop=1+ceil(max{log(2400)/(2log q), log(9600)/log(10/9)}).   (1)
Let m>=2600 be even. Take the2m-step prefix
h_pre=(1,463/135,1,131/30) repeated m/2 times.
Let k>=0 satisfy
k L_prop<=m-2600,
and append ANY prescribed finite nonnegative suffix(t_1,...,t_k).
There is one explicitly defined convex differentiable1-smooth function in R^(m+1), already fixed by m, which gives
W_(2m+k)(h_pre,t_1,...,t_k)>=(5/198)^2=25/39204.            (2)
Thus even arbitrary and unbounded suffix entries cannot repair this prefix within the stated linear number of steps. All constants are absolute and the threshold is independent of the suffix.
This is a restricted schedule theorem, not the unrestricted exact-profile target.

The fixed function used here differs from the one annihilated in50b0bb4cd67cac9f. Its gradient amplitudes grow by q and its negative correlation changes from-a_0 to-a=-a_0 q, leaving the actual prefix steps unchanged.

## 2. Explicit function and prefix data
In R^(m+1) construct
zeta_0=e_0,
zeta_(i+1)=-a zeta_i+sqrt(1-a^2)e_(i+1).
Put v_i=chi at even i and v_i=1 at odd i,
alpha_i=q^i v_i, omega_i=alpha_i zeta_i,
E_i=alpha_i^2,
L_i(x)=<omega_i,x>-kappa E_i,
J(x)=max{0,L_0(x),...,L_m(x)},
f(x)=min_z{J(z)+||x-z||^2/2}.
This gives one finite max-affine function with explicit nonnegative offsets and an explicitly real PSD Gram matrix
<omega_i,omega_j>=alpha_i alpha_j(-a)^|i-j|.
The vectors omega_i are independent.

The envelope is globally convex differentiable1-smooth: its unique proximal minimizer exists by the quadratic term, the optimality equation gives grad f(x)=x-prox_J(x), and subgradient monotonicity gives
||grad f(x)-grad f(y)||^2<=<grad f(x)-grad f(y),x-y>.
Its minimum is0 and its minimizer set is
P_min={x:L_i(x)<=0 for all i}.
For every x, its gradient belongs to conv{0,omega_0,...,omega_m}; more precisely it is a convex combination of normals active at prox_J(x).

Start at X_0=T omega_0. Set
b_i=T-1+(T/a)alpha_(i+1)/alpha_i
    =T-1+(T/a_0)v_(i+1)/v_i.
These are exactly463/135 at even i and131/30 at odd i, because the q factors cancel.
Define X_(i+1)=X_i-(1+b_i)omega_i. We now prove that these are the actual block starts, with both block-i queries having gradient omega_i.

## 3. Complete legality check of the prefix
Let Z_i=X_i-T omega_i,
B_i=(T/a)alpha_(i+1), D_i=B_i(1-a^2).
Then
Z_0=0, Z_(i+1)=Z_i-B_i(zeta_i+a zeta_(i+1)).
Taking inner products gives
<Z_i,zeta_j>=0 for j>=i,
<Z_i,zeta_j>=-S_(j,i) for j<i,
S_(j,i)=sum_(l=j)^(i-1)D_l(-a)^(l-j).                    (3)

Write delta=T-2=3/10 and Lambda=(T/a)(1-a^2)q.
The following exact rational inequalities hold:
4/5<theta^2<13/16,
theta chi-1<13/160,
Lambda<=437/900<39/80.
For the first two use9/10<theta<901/1000. For the third,
Lambda=(T/a_0)(1-a_0^2 q^2)<(T/a_0)(1-a_0^2)=437/900.
Define
S_floor=Lambda(1-theta chi)/(1-theta^2)<0.
Then
|S_floor|<(39/80)(13/160)/(3/16)=169/800.                 (4)

At a query X_i-u omega_i,u=0 or1, the proposed proximal point is
Z_i+d omega_i,d=T-u-1>=delta.
The terminal query X_m has d=T-1 and is also covered.
The current plane has value(d-kappa)E_i>=E_i/5>0.
For j<i, its deficit above plane j is
d alpha_i(alpha_i-C_ij alpha_j)+alpha_j S_(j,i)
 -kappa E_i+kappa E_j, C_ij=(-a)^(i-j);                  (5)
future indices use the same expression with S=0.

The coefficient of d is positive. At even separation, for a past j the ratio C_ij alpha_j/alpha_i is at most a_0^2 chi<1, and for a future j it is at most theta^2 chi<39/40. At odd separation C_ij<0. Hence d=delta is the worst endpoint.

Future comparisons at even separation are positive definite quadratic forms:
(delta-kappa)alpha_i^2+kappa alpha_j^2-delta C_ij alpha_i alpha_j>0,
since delta^2 C_ij^2<=delta^2 a^4<(9/100)(13/16)^2<4kappa(delta-kappa).
At odd separation all three displayed terms have nonnegative signs.

For the past, put ell=i-j and normalize the deficit by q^(2j). Write z=q^ell v_i>=1 and y=v_j in[1,chi]. Finite geometric sums, using v in[1,chi], give
S_(j,i)/q^j>=S_floor(1-theta^ell) if ell is even,
S_(j,i)/q^j>=S_floor if ell is odd.                       (6)
For odd ell, the cross term is positive, so the deficit is at least
(delta-kappa)z^2+kappa y^2+S_floor y
 >=3/10-(169/800)(6/5)=93/2000>0.
For even ell, the expression
(delta-kappa)z^2-delta a^ell z y
increases with z>=1. Replace z by v_i, and then replace a^ell by the larger theta^ell. The resulting lower bound is
F=(delta-kappa)v_i^2+kappa y^2
 -delta w v_i y+S_floor(1-w)y, w=theta^ell in[0,theta^2].
It is affine in w. At w=0 the preceding93/2000 bound applies. At w=theta^2 it increases with v_i and decreases with y on[1,chi], since
2/5-(3/10)(13/16)(6/5)>0,
and
6/25-(3/10)(4/5)=0
while the omitted S_floor term is strictly negative.
Its minimum is at v_i=1,y=chi and exceeds
43/125-(3/10)(13/16)(6/5)-(39/80)(13/160)(6/5)
=127/32000>0.
Thus all old, future and zero planes are controlled. The active-plane subgradient equation proves every prescribed gradient and the entire prefix.

This verification uses the signed sums themselves. They can be negative; no nonpositive-residual hypothesis is substituted.

The initial closest minimizer is y_star=kappa omega_0. For even j,
<omega_j,y_star>/ (kappa E_j)=(a/q)^j=a_0^j<=1,
and odd inner products are negative. Thus y_star belongs to P_min. Constraint0 is active and
X_0-y_star=(T-kappa)omega_0
is its positive normal. Squared-distance expansion proves that y_star is closest. Its squared distance is
(T-kappa)^2 E_0=(11/5)^2(6/5)^2=4356/625.                (7)

## 4. Exact endpoint affine values
Put
B_even=S_floor/chi,
B_odd=Lambda(chi-theta)/(1-theta^2),
A_even=-B_even-kappa,
A_odd=-B_odd-kappa,
C_end=T+B_even.
For even m, equation(3) gives at X_m:
L_j(X_m)/E_j=A_even+C_end theta^(m-j) if j is even,
L_j(X_m)/E_j=A_odd-chi C_end theta^(m-j) if j is odd.     (8)
These are obtained by summing the two-period geometric series; for an even j,
S_(j,m)/alpha_j=B_even(1-theta^(m-j)),
while an odd j has
S_(j,m)/alpha_j=B_odd+chi B_even theta^(m-j).

We need the following uniform bounds:
A_even>=19/270, |A_even|<1, |A_odd|<=22/25<1.             (9)
The last two follow from(4) and
B_odd<(39/80)(3/10)/(3/16)=39/50.
For the first, put z=q^2. Direct algebra gives
B_even(q)-B_even(1)
=(T/chi)(z-1)(a_0 z-chi)/(1-a_0^2 z^2)<=0,
because a_0z=theta<chi.
At q=1, B_even(1)=-23/135, so A_even>=23/135-1/10=19/270.

## 5. Affine guards annihilating every later direction
Fix an even index j with j+51<=m. Let
r_guard=chi a_0^51,
lambda_guard=1/(1+r_guard),
w_j=lambda_guard omega_j+(1-lambda_guard)omega_(j+51),
G_j=lambda_guard L_j+(1-lambda_guard)L_(j+51).
Then G_j is an affine lower bound for J.
The normal w_j is orthogonal to EVERY omega_l with l>=j+51:
the Markov products cancel because51 is odd and
(1-lambda_guard)alpha_(j+51)=lambda_guard alpha_j a^51.

Equation(8) therefore gives, with all decaying endpoint terms cancelling,
G_j(X_m)=q^(2j) d_guard,
d_guard=[chi^2 A_even-chi theta^51(B_odd+kappa)]/
        [1+chi a_0^51].                                (10)
Now theta^51<1/100: theta<1-99/1000 gives theta^51<exp(-5), while the degree6 Taylor sum for exp(5) exceeds100. Also a_0^51<1/100 and q^51<chi; for the latter,
q^51<=(1-51/10000)^(-1)<6/5,
using the binomial bound by the geometric series.
Consequently
d_guard>=[38/375-132/12500]/(253/250)
=1702/18975>2/25.                                      (11)
The guard normal has ||w_j||^2<=E_j, since both constituent normals have norm at most alpha_j (q^51<chi).

For later use, its inner products obey the explicit bounds
|<w_j,omega_l>|<=E_j a_0^(j-l) for l<j,
|<w_j,omega_l>|<=E_j theta^(l-j) for j<=l<j+51,
<w_j,omega_l>=0 for l>=j+51.                            (12)
To check the middle formula before taking absolute values, it is
lambda_guard alpha_j alpha_l(-a)^(l-j)
 [1-a^(2(j+51-l))].
For l<j replace that last factor by1-a^102 and use the corresponding Markov product. The amplitude factors then give (12).

## 6. A uniform lower bound on the proximal value after any late-direction perturbation
For r>=2600, let
V_r=span{omega_i:r<=i<=m}.
Take ANY x in X_m+V_r, with no bound on the coefficients of its displacement. Put g=grad f(x), z=x-g=prox_J(x).

Choose the largest even J_0 with J_0+51<=r. Take50 guards with starts
J_0, J_0-52,...,J_0-52*49.
All starts are nonnegative because r>=2600, and each guard is constant on X_m+V_r by its orthogonality. Let E_top=chi^2 q^(2J_0).
Their energies are at least(12/25)E_top, because
q^(-5096)>=1-5096/10000>12/25.
By(10)--(11), each guard value at x is at least E_j/18. Hence their average value is at least(2/75)E_top.

For any one normal omega_l, (12) bounds the sum of the50 absolute guard inner products by(100/99)E_top. Here is a direct justification of the uniform sum bound. The starts are52 apart, every guard to the left of l by at least51 contributes0, and at most one interval can contain l. At position d in[0,51] within that interval the remaining geometric bound is
theta^d+a_0^(52-d)/(1-a_0^52).
This is a convex function of d, so its maximum is at an endpoint. At d=0 it is1/(1-a_0^52)<100/99; at d=51 it is less than1/100+(9/10)/(1-1/100)<100/99. Before all intervals the single geometric-series bound is also<100/99.
Because g belongs to conv{0,omega_0,...,omega_m}, the average guard normal has inner product with g at most(2/99)E_top.

Since J(z) is at least the average guard value at z=x-g, we obtain
J(prox_J(x))>= (2/75-2/99)E_top=(16/2475)E_top.
Also r-J_0<=52 and q^(-104)>49/50, so
E_top>=(49/50)chi^2 q^(2r).
In particular
J(prox_J(x))> chi^2 q^(2r)/200.                         (13)
This holds for every x in the whole affine space X_m+V_r, independently of its displacement size. It is the key difference from a merely local covariance check.

## 7. Bounded backward propagation of active indices
Let E_r^ref=chi^2 q^(2r). For j<r, the Markov structure and(8) imply that at x in X_m+V_r there is one real scalar Q_x such that
L_j(x)/E_j=A_even+(chi/v_j)Q_x theta^(r-j) for even j,
L_j(x)/E_j=A_odd-(chi/v_j)Q_x theta^(r-j) for odd j.       (14)
For completeness one may take
Q_x=C_end theta^(m-r)+
    (-1)^r <zeta_r,x-X_m>/(chi q^r).
For j<r the inner product with any later normal factors through zeta_r, giving(14).

For ANY gradient g=grad f(x), the full Gram matrix gives
|<omega_j,g>|<=chi E_j,
because |<omega_j,omega_l>|/E_j is at most
chi theta^(l-j) for l>=j or chi a_0^(j-l) for l<j.
Thus for ell=r-j>0,
L_j(prox_J(x))/E_r^ref
 <=3q^(-2ell)+chi |Q_x| a_0^ell.                       (15)

If |Q_x|<10, (13) is a lower bound E_r^ref/200 for the active level.
If |Q_x|>=10, choose j in{r-1,r-2} with even parity for Q_x>0 or odd parity for Q_x<0. Equations(9),(14) and the bound on <omega_j,g> give
L_j(prox_J(x))
 >=E_j[(4/5)|Q_x|-11/5]
 >=(|Q_x|/2)E_j
 >=(|Q_x|/4)E_r^ref,                                   (16)
where the last inequality uses q^(-4)/chi^2>1/2.

By the definition(1),
q^(-2L_prop)<1/2400, a_0^L_prop<1/9600.
For ell>=L_prop, (15) is therefore< E_r^ref/400 if |Q_x|<10. If |Q_x|>=10, its value divided by |Q_x| is< E_r^ref/4000. In either case it is strictly below the active level furnished by(13) or(16).
Therefore no plane with index j<=r-L_prop is active at prox_J(x). The zero plane is not active either by(13). We conclude
grad f(x) belongs to span{omega_i:i>=r-L_prop}
for every x in X_m+V_r and r>=2600.                    (17)

This is a proved restriction on actual gradients of the one global function. It retains all old planes; the index exclusion comes from comparing their values with a certified positive active level.

## 8. Arbitrary suffix and normalized lower bound
Run any prescribed finite suffix steps from X_m. After t suffix steps, induction using(17) shows
x^(t) belongs to X_m+V_(m-t L_prop)
as long as m-t L_prop>=2600. Indeed each update adds a scalar multiple of a gradient in the displayed larger span; the scalar can be arbitrarily large.

The guard G_0 uses normals0 and51, and is orthogonal to every omega_i with i>=51. Hence it remains CONSTANT throughout every allowed suffix, with value d_guard>=2/25. Its normal satisfies ||w_0||^2<=chi^2=36/25.
To transfer this affine lower bound through the envelope, note that J is at least both0 and G_0. For any lambda in[0,1], completing the square gives
f(x)>=lambda G_0(x)-lambda^2||w_0||^2/2.
Take lambda=(2/25)/(36/25)=1/18. At the terminal point this yields
2f(x)>= (2/25)^2/(36/25)=1/225.
Divide by the exact initial squared distance4356/625 from(7):
2f(x)/||X_0-y_star||^2 >=(1/225)/(4356/625)
=25/39204=(5/198)^2.
This proves(2) for every allowed suffix, including zero steps and arbitrarily large finite entries.

Independence of the omega_i and their nonzero adjacent correlations also show nonseparability: isolate each zero/plane hinge, whose envelope Hessian is the rank-one projector onto omega_i. Adjacent such projectors do not commute. Thus no fixed orthonormal coordinates separate the function.

## 9. Scope and exact-profile relevance
This gives a full-suffix construction on a precise unbounded-horizon class, with a number of arbitrary repair steps proportional to the prefix length. It replaces, rather than assumes robustness of, the witness refuted by the two-step annihilator.

Using the verified benchmark bound B_n>=2n+1 from fact0be8b54e0a33381c, the candidate implies W_n/U_n^ZJ>= (25/39204)(2n+1) on this padded-prefix family, tending to infinity. That consequence remains conditional on verification of this new candidate.

The unrestricted problem is still open here. No assertion says an arbitrary near-optimal schedule contains this periodic prefix, or can be transformed to it without cost. The new reusable mechanism is the explicit affine guards and the finite backward propagation estimate(17), with all matrix, value, minimum and suffix conditions included.

## DOC-002675 — project/research/max_markov_relay_mutation.md

来源：D:\Danus\runtime\projects\gd-exact-profile-20260926\research\max_markov_relay_mutation.md

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002675 -->
# Correlated-frame mutation of the Moreau relay
Status: complete new analytic candidate; unverified. No verifier allocation requested.
This mutates11bf723a8eab9940 after its equal-distance orthogonal-state relay failed repeated(1,4). It retains an actual single max-affine Moreau envelope, with nonzero offsets, variable gradient energies and every supporting plane.

## 1. A globally specified nonstationary correlated family
Fix m>=1 and arbitrary b_i in[4,5],0<=i<m. Use the schedule
h=(1,b_0,1,b_1,...,1,b_(m-1)).
In R^(m+2) take an orthonormal basis e_0,...,e_(m+1). Fix r=-2/5 and set
u_0=e_0,
u_(i+1)=r u_i+sqrt(1-r^2)e_(i+1),0<=i<=m.                (1)
Then ||u_i||=1 and <u_i,u_j>=r^|i-j|, directly by induction. These are the block-start points, not independent sampled curvature matrices.

Put M_i=1+b_i for i<m, M_m=5, and
w_i=(u_i-u_(i+1))/M_i,0<=i<=m.
Choose eta=1/50 and define
J(z)=max{0, max_(0<=i<=m)(<w_i,z>-eta)},
f(x)=min_z {J(z)+||x-z||^2/2}.                           (2)
All planes and points are fixed by h. The Moreau envelope f is convex differentiable1-smooth, minimized at0 with value0, because J is finite nonnegative convex and J(0)=0. If plane i is active at z=x-w_i, then w_i belongs to the subdifferential of J at z, so this z is the unique minimizer in(2), grad f(x)=w_i and f(x)=<w_i,x>-||w_i||^2/2-eta. This follows directly from the subgradient optimality condition for the strongly convex minimization; monotonicity of the two subgradients gives the global1-Lipschitz gradient bound.

Start from x_0=u_0, so the initial squared distance from minimizer0 is1.
The intended two queries in block i are u_i and u_i-w_i, both with gradient w_i. Their two steps then end exactly at
u_i-(1+b_i)w_i=u_(i+1).                                 (3)

## 2. Closed-form comparisons with ALL old and future planes
Let x=u_i-(c-1)w_i, where1<=c<=2 for the two queries of a real block. The terminal query has i=m,c=1. Its candidate proximal point is z=u_i-c w_i.
Write t=c/M_i. Direct inner-product calculation from(1) gives
A_i(c):=<w_i,z>=(1-r)/M_i [1-2t],                         (4)
and for k>=1,
<w_(i+k),z>=(1-r)r^(k-1)/M_(i+k) [r+t(1-r)],              (5)
<w_(i-k),z>=(1-r)r^(k-1)/M_(i-k) [-1+t(1-r)].             (6)
Equations(4)--(6) check every plane; no nonadjacent interpolation condition is dropped.

All M_j lie in[5,6]. Equation(4) is at least
(7/5)(1/5-4/25)=7/125,
because the minimum over c occurs at c=2 and (M-4)/M^2 increases for5<=M<=6.
Thus the current affine plane is positive after subtracting eta=1/50.

For the other planes, divide their values and(4) by the positive factor(1-r)/M_i. Put a=2/5, so r=-a. For any real A,
max_(k>=1) r^(k-1) A=max{A,-a A}.
Therefore every future-plane value from(5), after this normalization, is at most the larger of
-2M_i/25+7c/25,
4M_i/125-14c/125.                                       (7)
Here we used M_i/M_j<=M_i/5 and the nonnegative maximal value; the maximum of the two displayed bounds has that sign.

Since -1+(7/5)t<0, the largest value in(6) occurs at lag2 if such a past plane exists. Every past-plane value is bounded above by
2M_i/25-14c/125.                                        (8)
A nonexistent lag2 only makes this upper estimate conservative.

The normalized current value is1-2c/M_i. Its differences from the three bounds in(7)--(8) are, respectively,
1+2M_i/25-c(2/M_i+7/25),
1-4M_i/125-c(2/M_i-14/125),
1-2M_i/25-c(2/M_i-14/125).
All three decrease with c over1<=c<=2. At c=2 each increases with M_i on[5,6], by direct differentiation. Their minimum values at M_i=5,c=2 are
1/25, 33/125, 3/125,
all strictly positive. This proves strict domination of every other nonzero plane. Equal offsets eta cancel in these comparisons.

Thus plane i is active at every indicated proximal point. The induction(3) is exact, and the terminal point is u_m with gradient w_m.

## 3. Exact nonvanishing objective
At the terminal query M_m=5, so
<w_m,u_m>=(1-r)/5=7/25,
||w_m||^2=2(1-r)/25=14/125.
Hence
f(u_m)=7/25-7/125-1/50=51/250,
and the legal normalized error is
W_(2m)(h)>=2f(u_m)=51/125.                               (9)
This holds uniformly over every m and every varying b_i in[4,5].

The two intermediate values in real block i are also explicit:
f(u_i-u w_i)=(1-r)/M_i-(2u+1)(1-r)/M_i^2-eta, u=0,1.
Together with(1)--(3), these give all actual points, values and gradients. Their complete interpolation legality follows from the displayed global function and was established by checking every supporting plane.

For L>0 use Lf; the prescribed h_t/L updates and normalized error are unchanged.

## 4. Growing dimension, temporal correlations and nonseparability
The stage gradient energies are2(1-r)/M_i^2, so they vary with the b_i. For j=i+k>i,
<w_i,w_j>=-(1-r)^2 r^(k-1)/(M_i M_j).                    (10)
They are nonzero at every separation and alternate in sign. The two equal gradients inside each block add positive temporal correlations. The Gram matrix is explicitly PSD because(1) constructs real vectors, not because of a guessed covariance condition.

The normals w_0,...,w_m are linearly independent: the coefficient of the new basis vector e_(i+1) in w_i is nonzero, while all earlier normals lack that coordinate. The number of independent sampled gradient directions therefore grows with m.

The Moreau envelope is genuinely nonseparable in any fixed orthonormal coordinates. For each i, linear independence of the normals allows a point z satisfying <w_i,z>=eta and <w_j,z><eta for j!=i. Around x=z+theta w_i,0<theta<1, the local envelope is that of the single hinge max{0,<w_i,z>-eta}, and its Hessian is the projector w_i w_i^T/||w_i||^2. Neighboring normals are neither orthogonal nor parallel by(1),(10), so these two projectors do not commute. A function separable in one orthonormal basis has commuting Hessians at all points where they exist. This proves the claim.

This family is thus outside both the scalar constructions and the exact orthogonality hypotheses of fact0e7e78056dc6258c.

## 5. The same schedules converge on every quadratic
For b in[4,5], the scalar quadratic block multiplier is
p_b(lambda)=(1-lambda)(1-b lambda),0<=lambda<=1.
For0<=lambda<=1/5 it is nonnegative and at most exp(-lambda). For1/5<=lambda<=1 its positive maximum is at most4/25, and its negative minimum has magnitude at most
max_(4<=b<=5)(b-1)^2/(4b)=4/5.
Consequently the entire quadratic-class normalized objective is at most
max{1/(2 e m),(4/5)^(2m)} ->0,                           (11)
uniformly in all the varying b_i. Orthogonal diagonalization extends this bound to every finite-dimensional convex quadratic. Equation(9) is therefore a nonlinear, nonseparable persistent-error construction on mixed short/long schedules whose full quadratic channel converges. It is not a claim that scalar nonlinear witnesses are small on this particular class.

## 6. Exact silver threshold within the stationary correlated relay
Now take a common block mass M>0 and common ordinary-block sum s>=0, so the checkpoint is b=M-s. Use M_i=M also for the terminal virtual normal, a common offset eta, and r=-a with0<=a<1. At the final ordinary query put t=(s+1)/M. Formula(4) has normalized current value C=1-2t.

The largest past-plane comparison is the lag2 value
a[1-t(1+a)].
It is dominated by C exactly when
t<=1/(2+a),                                             (12)
provided a<1. The largest future comparison is max{A,-a A}, where A=-a+t(1+a). Its lag1 requirement C>=A is exactly
t<=(1+a)/(3+a).                                         (13)
The other requirement C>=-a A is t<=(1+a)/(2+a), which is weaker than(12).
Thus every plane comparison, including arbitrary past and future lags, is satisfied precisely at
t<=min{1/(2+a),(1+a)/(3+a)}                              (14)
for a chain containing the relevant lag2 and future1 comparisons; in any shorter chain it is a sufficient condition. The zero plane additionally requires eta<= (1+a)(1-2t)/M, so a strictly positive common offset is available throughout(14).

The first term in(14) decreases with a, the second increases. Their crossing solves a^2+2a-1=0, namely a=sqrt2-1. The largest allowed t is therefore sqrt2-1, and the least admissible common mass ratio is
M/(s+1)=1+sqrt2.                                        (15)
This is an exact optimization INSIDE the stated stationary frame construction. It is not a profile comparison and not an optimality theorem for general GD. The special case a=0 needs M>=3(s+1), recovering the independent-state relay's restriction. The correlated choice repairs repeated(1,4), since M=5,s+1=2 and5/2>1+sqrt2.

For a varying block partition, formulas(4)--(6) remain explicit with the actual M_i and actual endpoint query values c=1,s_i+1. With nonnegative plane offsets eta_i, full legality is the finite system
A_i(c)-eta_i>=0,
A_i(c)-eta_i>=<w_j,u_i-cw_i>-eta_j for every j,
at those two c endpoints. The comparisons are affine in c, so they cover all intermediate nonnegative partial sums. This is a precise extension mechanism with all remote planes retained. The robust interval construction above is an actual solved nonstationary case of that system.

## 7. Target-scale limitation still visible
The threshold(15) alone does not imply that arbitrary schedules admit a strong relay decomposition. In particular it requires a selected checkpoint b to satisfy
b >= sqrt2 s+(1+sqrt2)
in the stationary case, so it does not capture arbitrary smaller checkpoints after large ordinary blocks. The intended lower-bound profile is not obtained by asserting that these decompositions always exist.

The next mutation must use variable block-start distances, nonstationary correlations, or within-block changing gradients while keeping the full comparisons(4)--(6) or their exact replacement. The concrete achievement here is a globally legal correlated family that repairs the previous relay's hostile(1,4) test, survives arbitrary parameter changes b_i in[4,5], and exposes an exact correlation-versus-history tradeoff. The all-schedule (1-o(1))U_n^ZJ inequality remains open.

# Minimizer-distance audit for the three Moreau relays
Status: complete explicit calculations, unverified; no new verifier call.
Responds to master_guidance aded2db8c57a7eae. These are restricted schedule constructions, not the exact minimax target.

## 1. The minimizer set and a projection certificate
For any finitely many vectors w_i and nonnegative offsets eta_i let
J(z)=max(0,max_i(<w_i,z>-eta_i)),
f(x)=min_z(J(z)+||x-z||^2/2).
The minimum defining f is attained because its nonnegative first term plus the quadratic is coercive. Thus f(x)=0 if and only if both nonnegative terms vanish, equivalently x=z and J(x)=0. Hence
argmin f = P := {z:<w_i,z><=eta_i for every i}.
In particular 0 is a minimizer but need not be closest to x_0.

A direct projection certificate is: y_proj belongs to P and
x_0-y_proj=sum_i lambda_i w_i, lambda_i>=0,
with lambda_i=0 unless <w_i,y_proj>=eta_i.
For any z in P, <x_0-y_proj,z-y_proj><=0; expanding ||x_0-z||^2 then proves ||x_0-z||^2>=||x_0-y_proj||^2. This proves the certificate without invoking an external projection theorem. A feasible y_proj alone gives the upper bound dist(x_0,P)^2<=||x_0-y_proj||^2, sufficient to strengthen a normalized lower bound.

For completeness, each use of a Moreau relay below is certified by this direct rule: if the indicated affine plane is active at z=x-w_i, then w_i is a subgradient of J there, so z is the unique minimizer and grad f(x)=w_i, f(x)=<w_i,x>-||w_i||^2/2-eta_i. Subgradient monotonicity proves that grad f is1-Lipschitz. Convexity follows by partial minimization, and uniqueness plus the two minimizing inequalities gives differentiability.

## 2. Original independent-state relay, unchanged
Take m>=2, 5<=b_i<=11/2, M_i=1+b_i, rho_i=1/M_i, w_i=rho_i(e_i-e_(i+1)) for i<m, w_m=e_m/6, eta_i=1/36, x_0=e_0.
The legal trajectory for h=(1,b_0,...,1,b_(m-1)) has two queries e_i,e_i-w_i, then reaches e_(i+1), and terminates at e_m with 2f(e_m)=1/4.
Here is a self-contained check. At the proximal points e_i-cw_i with1<=c<=2, the current inner product is rho_i-2c rho_i^2>=1/18. The next inner product is c rho_i rho_(i+1), using rho_m=1/6; their difference is rho_i[1-c(2rho_i+rho_(i+1))]>=0 because all rho's are<=1/6. The previous inner product is nonpositive; all others are0. Equal offsets cancel, and the current plane exceeds0. At the terminal proximal point(5/6)e_m, the terminal plane value is1/9 and every other plane is negative. This proves every prescribed gradient and the value1/8.

The point
y_proj=(1/2)e_0+(1/3)e_1+(1/6)e_2
lies in P: every positive neighboring coordinate difference is at most1/6, and rho_i<=1/6. The terminal coordinate is at most1/6 as well. Its squared distance from e_0 is7/18. Therefore
W_(2m)(h)>= (1/4)/(7/18)=9/14.
When b_0=b_1=5, the distance is EXACT: e_0-y_proj=3w_0+w_1 and both constraints are active. Later b_i may still vary in the stated interval. For m=1 the feasible point y_proj=(1/3)e_0+(1/6)e_1 gives distance17/36 and lower bound9/17, exact distance when b_0=5 by the same multiplier check.

## 3. Original Markov-state relay, unchanged
Take m>=2, r=-2/5, u_0=e_0, u_(i+1)=r u_i+sqrt(1-r^2)e_(i+1) through i=m. Let M_i=1+b_i in[5,6] for i<m and M_m=5. Set w_i=(u_i-u_(i+1))/M_i, eta_i=1/50, x_0=u_0.
The exact terminal value is 2f(u_m)=51/125.
To retain the full function certificate, at z=u_i-cw_i,1<=c<=2,
<w_i,z>=(1-r)/M_i[1-2c/M_i],
<w_(i+k),z>=(1-r)r^(k-1)/M_(i+k)[r+c(1-r)/M_i],
<w_(i-k),z>=(1-r)r^(k-1)/M_(i-k)[-1+c(1-r)/M_i].
After division by(1-r)/M_i, every other value is at most the maximum of
-2M_i/25+7c/25, 4M_i/125-14c/125, 2M_i/25-14c/125.
The differences from the current value1-2c/M_i are minimized at c=2,M_i=5 and are respectively1/25,33/125,3/125. The current inner product is at least7/125>1/50. Thus every supporting plane is checked and both gradients equal w_i. The checkpoint gives u_(i+1) exactly. The terminal query uses c=1,M_m=5 and gives the displayed terminal value.

Define
y_proj=(23/56)u_0+(49/120)u_1+(7/40)u_2+(1/168)u_3.
The four coefficients sum to1. Direct use of <u_i,u_j>=r^|i-j| gives
<y_proj,u_0>=11/40, <y_proj,u_1>=7/40, <y_proj,u_2>=3/40, <y_proj,u_3>=-1/40.
Consequently <y_proj,u_i-u_(i+1)>=1/10 for i=0,1,2. For i>=3 that inner product equals
(1-r)r^(i-3)(-1/40),
whose largest positive value is7/500<1/10.
Since M_i>=5, every constraint <w_i,y_proj><=1/50 holds. This includes the terminal plane.
Furthermore ||y_proj||^2=829/4200, so
||u_0-y_proj||^2=1-2(11/40)+829/4200=2719/4200.
Thus uniformly over every varying b_i in[4,5],
W_(2m)(h)>= (51/125)/(2719/4200)=8568/13595.
When M_0=M_1=M_2=5 (M_2 is the fixed terminal mass if m=2), this is the exact minimizer distance. Indeed
u_0-y_proj=(33/56)(u_0-u_1)+(19/105)(u_1-u_2)+(1/168)(u_2-u_3),
a positive combination of precisely the three active normals after multiplication of each coefficient by its M_i. No claim of exactness is made for arbitrary varying first masses; feasibility suffices for the lower bound.

## 4. Exact distance for the newest Markov-gradient model
This calculation is independent of the relay's history inequalities. Let unit directions zeta_i have correlations C_ij, positive amplitudes alpha_i, omega_i=alpha_i zeta_i, and suppose
C_ij alpha_j<=alpha_i for every distinct i,j.
Let eta_i=kappa alpha_i^2 for a common kappa>=0, and take x_0=t_0 alpha_0 zeta_0 with t_0>kappa. Then
y_proj=kappa alpha_0 zeta_0
belongs to P: <omega_j,y_proj>=kappa alpha_j alpha_0 C_0j<=kappa alpha_j^2=eta_j, using the hypothesis with i=j and j=0. Constraint0 is active, and x_0-y_proj=(t_0-kappa)omega_0 is a positive multiple of its normal. Hence EXACTLY
dist(x_0,P)^2=(t_0-kappa)^2 alpha_0^2.
For the parameters explicitly constructed in ee5ddfe0b5f7f1f5, t_0=201/100,alpha_0=100/201,kappa=1/200, so the distance is401/402. The unchanged terminal value301/400 therefore corresponds to the strengthened normalized error
(301/400)(402/401)^2.
This last lower-bound reading is conditional on that candidate's fully written trajectory proof, which remains unverified; the projection identity itself was proved here from the displayed hypotheses.

## 5. Retuning the first relay's offsets and terminal normal
There is also a strictly stronger explicit version of Section2, with the same block trajectories:
keep the nonterminal w_i=rho_i(e_i-e_(i+1)), use eta_i=1/18 for i<m, and replace the terminal data by
w_m=(2/3)e_m, eta_m=2/9.
All nonterminal comparisons except the final next-plane comparison are unchanged. The zero-plane comparison holds since rho-4rho^2>=1/18. At the last nonterminal query, the difference between the current and terminal affine planes is minimized at c=2 and equals
1/6-rho/3-4rho^2>=0
for rho in[2/13,1/6]; the expression decreases in rho and vanishes at1/6. Earlier terminal-plane inner products are0, so their values are negative.
At the terminal point e_m the proximal point(1/3)e_m has terminal-plane value0; every other plane is nonpositive. Thus grad f(e_m)=(2/3)e_m and f(e_m)=2/9.

For every m>=1,
y_proj=(2/3)e_0+(1/3)e_1
belongs to the new P. Every relevant positive coordinate difference is at most1/3; rho_i/3<=1/18, and the terminal condition is equality if m=1 or has left side0 if m>=2. Its squared initial distance is2/9. Therefore this new fixed legal nonlinear function proves
W_(2m)(1,b_0,...,1,b_(m-1))>=2
for every5<=b_i<=11/2.
If b_0=5 then e_0-y_proj=2w_0 and constraint0 is active, so2/9 is the exact distance and the normalized value of this instance is exactly2.

These calculations strengthen constant-level instability tests, not an arbitrary-schedule lower bound. Neither a minimizer projection nor these changes of offsets supply the missing uniform comparison to U_n^ZJ.

# Variable-energy Markov gradients with positive affine offsets
Status: complete new construction and exact quantitative test; unverified.
No new verifier allocation requested. This is a mutation of the fixed-radius relay, not a claim that its sufficient conditions hold for arbitrary h.

## 1. A finite max-affine construction with a closed history criterion
Partition a predetermined nonnegative schedule chronologically as
(block_0,b_0,...,block_(m-1),b_(m-1),block_m),
where block_i has sum s_i, following the project notation.
Choose positive amplitudes alpha_i and positive virtual budgets t_i with
t_i>=s_i+1,0<=i<=m.
Put
B_i^M=(s_i+b_i-t_i)alpha_i for0<=i<m,
r_i^M=-t_(i+1)alpha_(i+1)/B_i^M,
and require B_i^M>0 and -1<r_i^M<0.
Choose unit vectors recursively in R^(m+1):
zeta_0=e_0,
zeta_(i+1)=r_i^M zeta_i+sqrt(1-(r_i^M)^2)e_(i+1).
Define omega_i=alpha_i zeta_i. Their correlations are explicitly
C_ij=<zeta_i,zeta_j>=product_(l=min(i,j))^(max(i,j)-1)r_l^M
for i!=j, and C_ii=1.
This gives a real PSD Gram matrix and linearly independent gradients.

Let nonnegative offsets eta_i be chosen and define the ONE fixed function
J(z)=max{0,max_(0<=i<=m)(<omega_i,z>-eta_i)},
f(x)=min_z {J(z)+||x-z||^2/2}.                            (1)
Start at X_0=t_0 alpha_0 zeta_0. One may impose t_0 alpha_0=1 for exact initial normalization; otherwise divide the final payoff by(t_0 alpha_0)^2.
The function f is convex differentiable1-smooth, minimized at0 with value0. These facts follow directly from the Moreau minimization of the finite nonnegative convex J. In particular, if plane i is active at z=x-omega_i, then the subgradient optimality condition gives grad f(x)=omega_i and
f(x)=<omega_i,x>-||omega_i||^2/2-eta_i.                   (2)
This directly realizes all values; no interpolation theorem is being invoked as an unverified black box.

Define the intended block-start points by
X_(i+1)=X_i-(s_i+b_i)omega_i.
Set Z_i=X_i-t_i omega_i.
The following three EXPLICIT conditions are sufficient for every intended gradient and hence the exact supplied GD trajectory:
(a) D_i^M:=B_i^M(1-(r_i^M)^2) satisfies
    D_i^M>=|r_i^M|D_(i+1)^M,0<=i<m-1;                  (3)
(b) C_ij alpha_j<=alpha_i for every distinct i,j;      (4)
(c) with delta_i=t_i-s_i-1>=0,
    0<=eta_i<=delta_i alpha_i^2,
    eta_i-eta_j<=delta_i alpha_i(alpha_i-C_ij alpha_j)
    for every distinct i,j.                           (5)

These are conditions on a specified frame and specified affine offsets. Their feasibility at the target scale for arbitrary schedules is NOT assumed.

## 2. Why the entire history is retained
The defining r_i^M gives the exact recurrence
Z_0=0,
Z_(i+1)=Z_i-B_i^M(zeta_i-r_i^M zeta_(i+1)).              (6)
A direct induction using the Markov products gives
<Z_i,zeta_j>=0 for j>=i,
and, for j<i,
<Z_i,zeta_j>=-sum_(l=j)^(i-1)D_l^M C_(j,l).             (7)
To verify(7), note that for j<=i,
<zeta_i-r_i^M zeta_(i+1),zeta_j>
=(1-(r_i^M)^2)C_(j,i),
whereas it is zero for j>=i+1.

The sum on the right of(7) is nonnegative. Pair its terms at l=j,j+1, then j+2,j+3, and so on. Each even-position product C_(j,l) is positive, and the paired contribution equals
C_(j,l)[D_l^M-|r_l^M|D_(l+1)^M]>=0
by(3). A possible final unpaired term is positive. Thus
<Z_i,omega_j><=0 for j<i,
<Z_i,omega_j> = 0 for j>=i.                             (8)
No old plane has been discarded.

At a query in block i let u be the partial ordinary step sum already used, so0<=u<=s_i. The intended point is x=X_i-u omega_i, and
z=x-omega_i=Z_i+d omega_i,
d=t_i-u-1>=delta_i.
The current plane has value d alpha_i^2-eta_i>=0.
By(8), any other plane has value at most
d alpha_i C_ij alpha_j-eta_j.
Its difference from the current plane is therefore at least
d alpha_i(alpha_i-C_ij alpha_j)-eta_i+eta_j.
Condition(4) allows replacing d by its lower bound delta_i; then(5) proves nonnegativity. Consequently plane i dominates all other planes and the zero plane at every query. Formula(2) gives exactly the intended constant gradient throughout block_i, including its checkpoint query. The actual update reaches X_(i+1), completing the induction. Repeated points from zero steps cause no difficulty.

The terminal ordinary block follows omega_m in the same way. Its exact normalized value is
W_n(h)>=
 [2(t_m-s_m-1/2)alpha_m^2-2eta_m]/(t_0 alpha_0)^2.      (9)
Every sampled value is explicitly
f(X_i-u omega_i)=(t_i-u-1/2)alpha_i^2-eta_i.
The construction is a finite-dimensional global function, so every cross-time smooth-convex interpolation inequality and the optimum sample are satisfied.

The amplitude recursion can be written
alpha_(i+1)=|r_i^M| (s_i+b_i-t_i)alpha_i/t_(i+1).          (10)
This displays the multiplicative transfer and its actual losses. The history price is(3)--(5), not an omitted all-schedule comparison.

## 3. A genuinely nonstationary solved parameter range
Fix m>=1. Let every nonterminal ordinary block be the single unit step, and let
4<=b_i<=17/4,0<=i<m.
The terminal ordinary block is empty. Thus n=2m.

Choose
delta=1/100,
t_i=2+delta=201/100 for EVERY i, including i=m,
alpha_0=100/201,
alpha_m=1/2,
and choose the interior amplitudes arbitrarily in[49/100,1/2].
The initial amplitude also lies in this interval. Set
eta_i=(delta/2)alpha_i^2=alpha_i^2/200.
All offsets are strictly positive, and the squared gradient norms alpha_i^2 can vary independently through this interval, subject only to the stated initial and terminal values.

For nonterminal i,
B_i^M=(b_i-1-delta)alpha_i.
The elementary bounds
29/20 <= B_i^M <=13/8
hold: the actual lower endpoint is(299/100)(49/100)>29/20 and the actual upper endpoint is(324/100)(1/2)<13/8.
Furthermore
|r_i^M|=(201/100)alpha_(i+1)/B_i^M
 <=(201/200)/(29/20)=201/290<7/10.                       (11)
It is strictly positive, so every new direction has a nonzero independent innovation.

The history weights satisfy
D_i^M=B_i^M(1-|r_i^M|^2)
 >=(29/20)(1-49/100)=1479/2000.                          (12)
For the upper bound use
D_i^M=B_i^M-[(201/100)alpha_(i+1)]^2/B_i^M.
Since(201/100)alpha_(i+1)>=9849/10000>49/50 and B_i^M<=13/8,
D_i^M <=13/8-(49/50)^2/(13/8)<21/20.                    (13)
The final strict comparison follows from
(49/50)^2/(13/8)=19208/32500>23/40.
Combining(11)--(13),
|r_i^M|D_(i+1)^M <(7/10)(21/20)=147/200
 <1479/2000<=D_i^M.
Thus the complete-history condition(3) holds uniformly in all independent choices of b_i and interior alpha_i.

For distinct indices, |C_ij|<(7/10)^|i-j|<=7/10.
In particular C_ij alpha_j<=7/20<49/100<=alpha_i, proving(4).
For all distinct i,j,
alpha_i(alpha_i-C_ij alpha_j)
 >=(49/100)^2-(7/10)(1/2)^2
 =651/10000.                                           (14)
For nonterminal phases delta_i=delta; for the terminal phase delta_m=1+delta, so always delta_i>=delta. On the other hand
eta_i-eta_j <=(delta/2)[(1/2)^2-(49/100)^2]
 =99delta/20000 <651delta/10000.
Together with eta_i=delta alpha_i^2/2<=delta_i alpha_i^2, this proves(5).

The function(1) therefore realizes the whole nonstationary schedule. The exact initial norm is t_0 alpha_0=1. The terminal point is X_m and alpha_m=1/2, giving
2f(X_m)
=2(201/100-1/2)(1/4)-2(1/200)(1/4)
=301/400.                                               (15)
Hence every schedule in this parameter range has the explicitly constructed legal lower bound
W_(2m)(1,b_0,...,1,b_(m-1))>=301/400
for all m>=1.

This improves the previous relay's quantitative test while using variable gradient energies, nonstationary correlations and positive affine offsets. No randomness is needed; randomly applying a common orthogonal rotation gives an equivalent legal law with the same payoff, without changing its strength.

## 4. Nonseparability and the quadratic comparison
The gradients omega_0,...,omega_m are linearly independent by their nonzero fresh coordinates in the Markov recursion. Adjacent inner products are
<omega_i,omega_(i+1)>=alpha_i alpha_(i+1)r_i^M,
which are nonzero. The global function is not separable in any fixed orthonormal basis: isolation of the face between the zero plane and each plane i yields open smooth regions whose Hessians are the distinct rank-one projectors onto span(omega_i). The normals are independent and adjacent ones are nonorthogonal, so these projectors do not commute. Simultaneous diagonal Hessians would be necessary for orthogonal separability.

For the same schedules, the full quadratic-class normalized error tends uniformly to0. On a scalar eigenvalue lambda in[0,1], each block multiplier is
(1-lambda)(1-b_i lambda).
For0<=lambda<=1/5 it is nonnegative and at most exp(-lambda). On[1/5,1] its absolute value is at most4/5, because b_i belongs to[4,17/4], a subset of[4,5], and the negative minimum magnitude(b_i-1)^2/(4b_i) is at most4/5 while the positive endpoint bound is4/25. Therefore the final normalized quadratic error is at most
max{1/(2 e m),(4/5)^(2m)}.
Spectral diagonalization proves the same statement in every finite dimension. This does not claim that all scalar nonlinear instances are controlled; it isolates a real nonlinear, nonseparable adversary with an explicit full-history certificate.

## 5. Relation to the earlier failed local AR rule
The key addition is the signed-sum formula(7) and its global control by(3), together with positive face margins and the explicit cross-plane inequalities(4)--(5). Choosing only a locally valid AR covariance would not prove them.

For example, the old constant-energy rule on the single-step schedule(2,3) corresponds formally to ordinary sums0, budgets t_i=1, amplitudes1, and correlations -1 and-1/2. It is already degenerate at the first correlation. Its history coefficients would be D_0^M=0 and D_1^M=3/2, violating the paired condition0>=3/2. Thus the old nonadjacent failure is detected rather than repeated.

## 6. Remaining target obligation
Equations(9)--(10) are a genuine multiplicative high-dimensional transfer with full legality conditions. The present solved range is a stability/instability test for mixed short/long schedules, not the exact minimax theorem. No claim is made that a partition, amplitudes, virtual budgets and correlations meeting(3)--(5) with payoff(1-o(1))U_n^ZJ exist for every h.

The conditions reveal specific costs: the virtual budget t_i must cover every ordinary-block query; the history weights D_i^M must control alternating past sums; and positive offsets must remain below the actual face margin. The next search is to vary these quantities on the recursive hostile schedules, or to replace the paired sufficient condition by a sharper exact signed-sum analysis. Simply postulating the needed choice would be theorem-strength and remains BLOCKED.
